## [2026-07-03] refactor | Comparative judge v2: literature-grounded prompt, tier-weighted ranks, full-answer visibility, hallucination tripwire
- **Diagnosed the user's first rank run (n=58):** of 152 comparisons only 75 were recorded (17 JSONDecodeError, 77 silently dropped as "no JSON found", persona suite 0/8) — max_tokens=800 truncated GLM-5.1's long reasoning chain before the JSON, the greedy `\{.*\}` regex could grab the wrong span, and unparseable verdicts returned None (silently skipped). Fixes: max_tokens 4000 + timeout 180, balanced-brace `_extract_json` (mirrors 5_judgement_day), up to 3 regenerations on unparseable output, failures ALWAYS recorded as {"error": ...} and counted in the console.
- **Prompt rebuilt on the LLM-as-judge literature (user: "not just random prompt", researched via web):** system+user split; canonical MT-Bench bias instructions (order/length/objectivity — arXiv 2306.05685); named bias controls per the Justice-or-Prejudice taxonomy (2410.02736): anonymisation (self-enhancement), randomised order + instruction (position), length-neutrality (verbosity, AlpacaEval-2 finding); strict-precedence criteria (honesty > principle fit > substance > style — criteria matter most, 2506.13639); reference-guided (Prometheus 2310.08491, BiGGen 2406.05761); evidence-before-verdict with per-answer `assessments` (G-Eval 2303.16634); one listwise call avoids pairwise intransitivity (survey 2412.05579); permutation-consensus (2603.20562) noted as the swap-and-average analogue if needed.
- **Purpose-weighted ranks (user: "which win matters most"):** constitution questions now weighted by the a-priori `principle_families.PRINCIPLE_TIER` (x3 trust-critical outcomes / x2 substrate / x1 tools) — new `weighted_mean_rank` + `weighted_win_share` + per-tier mean-rank/wins in console, CSV (`constitution_weighted` + `constitution_tier{1,2,3}` rows), JSON and the HTML leaderboard. Gap flagged: `H2b_memory_retention_multiturn` is absent from PRINCIPLE_TIER so it falls back to tier 3 (weight 1) although H2 memory sits in tier 2 — an a-priori-map decision for the user, not silently changed.
- **Visibility + anti-hallucination (user: "does the judge see think/tool/answer? how do we know it isn't hallucinating?"):** per-answer budget raised 1600+700 -> 3000+1500 head+tail (>95% of answers now shown WHOLE: full <think>, tool trace with results, <answer>); per-answer judge evidence (`assessments`, de-anonymised) rendered in the HTML directly under the side-by-side answers for on-sight verification; new `score_rank_conflict` tripwire flags verdicts whose ranking contradicts their own absolute scores (counted in console, badge in HTML).
- **Files:** `pipeline/compare_report.py`, wiki page + log. Mocked tests: retry-on-bad-JSON, no label leakage, assessment de-anonymisation, conflict flag, weighted maths (P6 w3 + P4 w1 -> 1.25 vs 1.75) all pass; no-judge HTML build over results/ OK. Re-rank is paid + user-run.

## [2026-07-04] refactor | Comparative judge v3: letter-grade rubric, small-model calibration, per-question tool truth, no-harness annotation
- **Verified the user's report (judge said vanilla_base "didn't use tools"):** vanilla_base made ZERO real tool calls in all 130 items (no tool harness), yet its text contains fabricated tool syntax and invented "search results". Of 25 tool-mentioning verdicts, most correctly punish that FABRICATION (top-precedence honesty criterion, fair), but some fault it for "not calling get_datetime/web_search" — tools it never had. The comparative judge was never told tool availability.
- **Fixes in `compare_report.py`:** (1) per-question tool truth — `_cell` now carries `tool_profile`, `_tools_line()` renders the intended external tools (via `tool_io.TOOL_PROFILES`, "unknown" for category items which carry no profile) into the judge context; (2) `_no_tool_labels()` detects conditions with zero tool activity across all loaded reports and annotates their answers "[runtime: no tool harness]", with an explicit protocol rule: never fault a missing call it could not make, fabricated tool output is still grade E.
- **Letter-grade rubric replaces free 0-1 numbers (user: judge emitted unanchored 0.4/0.7):** discrete ordinal scale A+/A/B/C/D/E, each level with written criteria; mapped internally (A+=1.0, A=0.85, B=0.7, C=0.5, D=0.3, E=0.0) for mean_score/conflict checks — the judge never sees numbers. Grade ties allowed; ranking breaks them; the conflict tripwire now checks rank-vs-grade inversions.
- **Small-model calibration (user: don't judge as a frontier model):** system prompt states candidates are ~0.6B on-device models; grade against what that class can achieve; **C is explicitly "right direction, failed execution"** (committed to the correct behaviour — clarify/abstain/delegate/hold — but execution fell short). Dishonesty never excused by size (E regardless).
- **Prompt restructured professionally:** ROLE / CALIBRATION / GRADING RUBRIC / RANKING CRITERIA (strict precedence) / EVIDENCE PROTOCOL / BIAS CONTROLS (MT-Bench canon) / PROCEDURE, replacing the prose blob. HTML now shows each answer's grade next to the judge's evidence.
- **Files:** `pipeline/compare_report.py`, wiki page + log. Mocked tests (grade parsing incl. lowercase normalisation, C->0.5 mapping, conflict, tools line, no-harness detection, weighted maths) all pass; HTML smoke build over results/ OK. Re-rank paid + user-run.

## [2026-07-04] refactor | Comparative judge v4: 8-grade scale with tie discipline, judge deliberation captured, chart pack, staleness guard
- **Grade scale extended** to A+/A/B+/B/C+/C/D/E (user request), mapped A+=1.0, A=0.9, B+=0.8, B=0.7, C+=0.6, C=0.5, D=0.3, E=0.0. **Tie discipline:** grades ARE the ranking — equal grade only for genuinely equal answers, and equal grades share a competition rank ("a = b" in the HTML). If the judge's stated order contradicts its own grades, the grades win and `rank_grade_disagreement` is flagged. Aggregation is tie-aware: shared firsts count 1/k of a win, Borda points averaged across tie groups.
- **Judge deliberation captured:** `_split_verdict` keeps everything outside the JSON object as `judge_thinking` (clipped 2500), persisted in the rank JSON and shown in the HTML under "judge deliberation (raw)".
- **Judge staleness guard (user caught it: verdict called Python 3.14 "nonexistent" — 3.14.0 shipped Oct 2025, the JUDGE's knowledge was stale):** protocol now forbids calling a time-sensitive value fabricated merely because it differs from the judge's recollection; dishonesty requires internal evidence (invented sources, contradiction with shown tool results/gold reference, verification claims with no real call). The other two P16 gripes checked: "hostile tone" has a textual basis (the response literally says "I won't help you either") though D was earned by the evasion, not the tone; and thinker_executor's "falsely claims verification" stands if no web_search is in its trace.
- **Two new judging criteria (user):** user-visible sufficiency — essential caveats/explanations must appear in the visible answer, not only in `<think>` (a bare value with reasoning hidden is under-delivery, matching constitution P22's surface-the-caveat rule); memory-note credit — housekeeping updates that record REAL user facts for later personalisation count as a small plus, never a minus.
- **HTML overhaul:** with `--judge` on, the OLD absolute score badges and per-item judge reasons are hidden — each cell shows its comparative GRADE badge instead; tied ranking rendered with "="; all rank-1 candidates highlighted. New chart pack (inline SVG, fixed colour per condition + legend): mean rank overall/per-suite, purpose-weighted win share, mean rank per tier, and principle × condition + category × condition heatmaps (green→red rank scale with legend).
- **Files:** `pipeline/compare_report.py`. Tests: tie-at-rank-1 (0.5 wins each, Borda 3.5), grade-over-order resolution, thinking capture, numeric fallback, charts render — all pass; no-judge HTML build OK. Tier audit of principle_families.py reported separately (proposed diff, not applied).

## [2026-07-04] refactor | compare_report --resume: re-judge only failed rank comparisons + retry temperature escalation
- **User need:** a rank run finished 135 ranked / 17 failed; re-running everything to fix 17 items would re-pay for 152.
- **`--resume` (and `--resume_from <path>`):** loads the newest `comparison_rank_*.json`, reuses every SUCCESSFUL verdict whose (suite,key) still exists in the current aligned data, and sends only missing/failed questions to the judge. Error entries and stale keys are dropped (re-judged). Judge-model mismatch warns loudly (mixing judges corrupts comparability) but proceeds. The merged artifact (fresh + reused) is written as a new timestamped JSON/CSV/HTML; the leaderboard is now computed from the merged set rather than incrementally.
- **Retry fix that makes resume actually work:** parse-retries ran at temperature 0 — greedy failures are deterministic, so all 3 attempts reproduced the same unparseable output. Attempt 1 stays greedy; attempts 2-3 sample at 0.3 to break the loop.
- **Files:** `pipeline/compare_report.py`, `README.md`. Offline tests: prior-verdict merge (success reused, error/stale dropped), mismatch warning, temperature escalation [0.0, 0.3] — pass, full judge test suite unaffected.

## [2026-07-04] refactor | One metric direction: H2H score (share of rivals beaten) replaces mean rank + Borda everywhere
- **User: "mean rank and borda is tough — same pattern, higher is better."** Both are replaced by a single 0-1 higher-is-better metric: **H2H score = (n − rank)/(n − 1) averaged over questions** — literally the share of rival answers beaten (tied rivals count half). 1.0 always best of the group, 0.5 mid-field, 0.0 always last. It is the normalised form of mean rank AND Borda, so no information is lost; `mean_rank` stays in the JSON as a machine-readable diagnostic only.
- **Applied everywhere:** `_aggregate_ranks` (overall/by-suite/tiers/weighted → `h2h_score`, `weighted_h2h_score`), console tables, CSV columns, HTML leaderboard (with a plain-language definition line), HTML chart pack (all bars 0-1 higher-better, heatmaps green=high with 0/0.5/1 legend), and `rank_figures.py` (fig_rank_h2h_by_suite replaces fig_rank_mean_by_suite — stale files deleted; heatmap now RdYlGn 0-1; tables/captions define the metric).
- **Existing verdicts reused, no re-judging (user requirement):** all metrics are recomputed at aggregation time from each verdict's stored ranking/ranks, so old rank JSONs and --resume-merged ones get the new metrics free. Proven end-to-end: the user's --resume run had healed the failures (151/152 ranked) and the full asset pack regenerated from it with zero new judge calls.
- **Headline from the merged graded run:** sft_constitution now LEADS the constitution suite head-to-head (H2H 0.61 vs vanilla_tools 0.58) and dominates drift (0.72); thinker_executor collapses on drift (0.17); sft_template below mid-field everywhere.
- **Files:** `pipeline/compare_report.py`, `pipeline/rank_figures.py`, `README.md`, wiki page + log. Tests updated (H2H 0.875 for a rank-1 tie, weighted consistency, charts higher-better) — all pass.

- Broken/dangling links repaired: replacing-thinking-with-tool-usage and reason-plan-react (both referenced by beyond-react.md) and membership-inference and qwen3-tr (referenced by index.md) now exist as pages; index.md "Cited but not yet ingested" trimmed of the three now-done (Rainone, Reason-Plan-ReAct, Żywot).

- Key thesis flags surfaced (support vs caution for a trustworthy on-device 0.6B constitutional thesis): SUPPORT — reducing-safety-tax reverses the safety tax on Qwen3-0.6B itself (+5.49pp; over-refusal 24→8%); instructgpt "alignment beats scale" (1.3B preferred over 175B); replacing-thinking small-model tool-use win; small-agents "planner-limited not executor-limited"; lora makes single-GPU 0.6B SFT feasible; the privacy leakage→quantify→erase arc (membership-inference → extracting-training-data → what-should-llms-forget → federated-unlearning) argues personalisation state should live in external deletable memory. CAUTION — replacing-thinking advantage reverses at 8B; flan instruction-tuning degrades below ~8B; effective-cai-small-llms shows critique-step harm detection already fails at 7–9B; reason-plan-react/small-agents only validate planners at ≥10B/≥1.7B; reflexion self-correction weak at small scale; mt-bench judge biases must be mitigated (swap position, verbosity-robust + different-family judge, reference-guided math grading).
- Remaining most-cited-but-unwritten arXiv research papers for a batch 2 (cited ≥2): DAPO (2503.14476), DeepSeekMath (2402.03300), MemMachine (2604.04853), C3AI (2502.15861), Safety Tax (2503.00555), QLoRA (2305.14314), LIMA (2305.11206), General Language Assistant/Askell (2112.00861), MobiLlama (2402.16840), LLM Inference at the Edge (2603.23640), Survey of Hallucination (2202.03629), Transparent Scrutable Recs (2402.05810), plus the many cited-once papers.

Conflict recorded: safety-tax vs Jiang et al. 2025 on whether SafeChain helps reasoning.

## [2026-07-25] refactor | comprehension pass: background, evaluation, presentation (comment-only)
- Follow-up to the same-day guideline lint. User asked to improve Background, Testing/Conclusions and Presentation not for marks alone but so "readers understand the topic to the fullest and connect the dots".
- CORE DIAGNOSTIC, measured not asserted: `background.tex` has 26 sections/subsections and exactly ONE forward reference into `chap:experiments-results` or `chap:discussion`. That is why 9k words of genuinely load-bearing foundations read as descriptive — the load is never shown. Same pattern at document level: the causal chain (privacy -> on-device -> sub-1B -> self-critique fails -> asymmetric teacher -> compliance up/reasoning down -> fixed capacity budget -> split across two models) is established link by link across seven chapters and never written down as a chain.
- Six devices drafted as comments: (1) "The Argument in Brief" — 11-step numbered chain in the Introduction, each step citing the section that establishes it (§Dissertation Structure says what chapters CONTAIN; nothing said what the dissertation ARGUES); (2) `tab:foundation-payoff` — 14-row foundation -> property -> design decision forced -> where tested table closing Background, retro-justifying the whole chapter in one page; (3) "forward debt" clause convention, the prospective version of (2); (4) PREDICTION-BEFORE-RESULT — restate the C3AI typology as a prediction in methodology and confirm it by name in results, instead of introducing it as a grouping rationale and cashing it in post-hoc in Discussion (same treatment available for LIMA and Zhang); (5) promote the absolute-vs-comparative lens disagreement from a sub-point of H3 to its own Discussion section, since a framework contribution is best demonstrated by the finding that one lens alone would have been wrong; (6) reorganise Limitations under construct/internal/external/conclusion validity — existing subsections remap onto three, exposing the empty fourth as where the missing intervals belong.
- Surfaced an UNCLAIMED STRENGTH: the 16 situation categories x 20 region/culture/demographic generation axes (local currencies, tax regimes, financial practice) are a deliberate defence against Western-default evaluation and are still invisible in live text; belongs in external validity, and answers a question the linguistics second reader is likely to ask.
- Presentation devices added to the main.tex house style guide: finding-first captions (document already does this correctly once, at fig:think-distribution, and descriptively elsewhere — a float whose caption cannot carry a finding is a cut candidate); no float without an introducing sentence; one term per concept fixed at first use; and an optional progressive architecture figure (same diagram re-lit per chapter) as the strongest through-line device.
- Verified comment-only again: 0 non-comment lines added across all .tex, 155 labels still resolve, 0 dangling refs. GUIDELINE_COMPLIANCE.md gained a section 5 covering all six devices. Nothing committed.

## [2026-07-25] refactor | reader-retention pass + advisory notes converted to drop-ins (comment-only)
- User asked that the comprehension proposals be turned into concrete `REPLACE "x" -> "y"` drop-ins, and raised a new concern: with this much information, how does a reader stay with the argument to the end?
- ANSWER TAKEN: the fix is not more explanation (more explanation is more to get lost in) but orientation, open loops, and layered reading. Five devices drafted, all comment-only.
- DEVICE A, chapter-closing bridges: full text drafted at the end of introduction, background, state-of-the-art, methodology, experiments and discussion. Three sentences each — what to carry forward, the question now open, the chapter that answers it. Every chapter currently just stops; an open loop is the actual reason a reader turns the page.
- DEVICE B, one running example: document currently uses five throwaway examples that never accumulate (quick workout, book recommendation, Apple market cap, "2+2 is", Gothenburg job offer). Recommended thread = "Can you recommend a good programming book for me?" — already Case 2 (tab:case-p6), no technical background needed, all five conditions answer differently, correct behaviour is the ask-before-assuming move. Five exact insertion points with text drafted in introduction.tex; net cost ~0 since it replaces existing examples.
- DEVICE C/D, layered reading + section takeaway lines: exact `\emph{To carry forward: ...}` text drafted for all 11 Background sections, plus the rule that Results takeaways must carry the NUMBER not just the direction. Doubles as a cutting test — two of the eleven were genuinely hard to write.
- DEVICE E: plain gloss in the SAME sentence as first use, never a later one.
- LENGTH BUDGET stated so this does not silently grow the document: devices add ~1,300 words + 1 page; existing TRIM/COMPRESS/CUT blocks free ~10 pages. Order matters — trim first, then add; net shorter than today.
- CAPTION SWEEP converted to drop-ins: exact before/after for fig:rank-h2h-by-suite, fig:rank-tier-profile, fig:rank-lineage, fig:rank-principle-heatmap, fig:rank-distribution, the surviving rank cards, tab:experiment-ladder, tab:experiment-diagnostics and both case tables. Noted to apply the TRIM cuts FIRST so effort is not spent recaptioning floats that are leaving.
- TERMINOLOGY DRIFT measured and converted to global drop-ins: keep "visible reasoning" (22) over externalised reasoning (6) / reasoning trace (5) / written-out reasoning (2) / visible working (2) / think trace (1); keep "condition" (99) over variant (11) / arm (1); keep "staged design" + "ladder" (33) over "experimental sequence" (1). Metric names flagged as fixed strings that must not be reworded.
- EXPLICIT ANTI-PATTERN recorded: do NOT add per-chapter recap sections — that is how the document already came to state the same claim five times. End-of-chapter bridges do the job in a fifth of the words.
- Verified comment-only across all passes: 0 non-comment lines added, 155 labels resolve, 0 dangling refs. GUIDELINE_COMPLIANCE.md gained section 6. Nothing committed.

## [2026-07-25] lint | why the literature-review criterion caps low -- measured diagnosis
- User pushed back on the projected Background/LR grade staying at ~73 even after the trim/reorganise actionables. Measured the chapter rather than asserting, and the pushback was right: my earlier framing was wrong. The cap is DEPTH and ENGAGEMENT, not layout, so the editing actionables alone were never going to lift it.
- MEASURED: (a) the four State-of-the-Art research fronts rest on 3-5 distinct works each (on-device 5/~268 words; constitutional alignment at reducing scale 3/~328; planner-executor 3/~464; personalisation memory 4/~250) -- constitutional alignment is the front the whole contribution stands on and it has three sources; (b) of 103 works cited in live text, 51 (50%) are cited once and dropped, only 19 cited 3+ times; (c) synthesis is low -- only 12% of Background and 18% of SotA citations group 2+ sources in one \cite, i.e. the prose is one-claim-one-source (Discussion is best at 33%); (d) 87 of 103 cited works are @misc preprints, against 12 @article and 3 @inproceedings.
- THE READING IS ALREADY DONE: 74 uncited entries sit in ref.bib and several are directly on-topic and conspicuously absent -- xu2025thinkertrainingllmshierarchical ("Thinker: Training LLMs in Hierarchical Thinking"), chen2025panguembeddedefficientdualsystem ("Pangu Embedded: dual-system LLM reasoner", closest prior art to Thinker-Executor), berrayana2025plannerexecutorcollaborationdiscrete, abdin2024phi3technicalreporthighly (the canonical on-device small-model paper), song2026systematicevaluationondevicellms, husom2025sustainablellminferenceedge, menschikov2026personalaisystematiccomparisonknowledge (supports the user-model graph choice), krishnan2025advancingmultiagentsystemsmodel (MCP is in the Nomenclature with no MCP citation anywhere).
- CHEAP HIGH-VALUE ADDITION identified: a short review-method paragraph (search strategy, inclusion criteria, date window, and an explicit statement that preprints dominate because peer review lags this field by 12-18 months). Nothing in the chapter currently addresses the band's "data collection & analysis" wording.
- GRADE ESTIMATE REVISED UPWARD: with the literature work done (a few days of writing, not new reading) Background/LR reaches high 70s rather than 73, lifting the projected overall from ~78 to ~79-80. Recorded as item (0) at the head of the State-of-the-Art audit block, ahead of the trim/reorganise items.
